# StartEx Complete Code Review Questions

Total questions: **571**

## Contents

- 1. Review method, scope, and evidence (10 questions)
- 2. Product boundaries, operating modes, and side-effect separation (15 questions)
- 3. Build system, dependency integrity, repository configuration, and release artifacts (25 questions)
- 4. Architecture, process composition, state ownership, and lifecycle (18 questions)
- 5. Android manifest, platform security, compatibility, and operating-system behavior (20 questions)
- 6. Visual system, shared Compose components, navigation, accessibility, and honest state presentation (24 questions)
- 7. Screen-by-screen behavior, validation, navigation, and accessibility (42 questions)
- 8. Wallet lifecycle, secret handling, Android Keystore, biometrics, and trusted authorization (40 questions)
- 9. Provider integrations, HTTP and WebSocket transport, parsing, freshness, retries, and redaction (41 questions)
- 10. Foreground monitoring service, session state machine, recovery, concurrency, and position protection (35 questions)
- 11. Candidate discovery, observation, quote validation, token safety, hard filters, and deterministic scoring (49 questions)
- 12. Independent risk controller, circuit breakers, limits, configuration defaults, and editable ranges (28 questions)
- 13. Paper entry execution, position valuation, exit decisions, retries, and accounting (36 questions)
- 14. Manual mainnet SOL transfer, trusted destination enforcement, write-ahead persistence, submission uncertainty, and confirmation (41 questions)
- 15. Future Live transaction parser, validator, allowlisting, and uncertain-transaction reconciliation scaffold (18 questions)
- 16. Room schema, DAOs, repository transactions, retention, DataStore, export, and privacy (42 questions)
- 17. Device health, entry policy, notifications, alert delivery, and operational event logging (18 questions)
- 18. Automated tests, device verification, CI workflows, scanners, exceptions, and evidence quality (33 questions)
- 19. Known hotspots, maintainability, performance, documentation, and final integrated verification (36 questions)

# 1. Review method, scope, and evidence

## Q001. Apply the source-of-truth hierarchy correctly

Review whether implementation decisions and review conclusions follow the project's evidence hierarchy: current Kotlin, XML, and Gradle source first; generated Room schema, merged manifest, resolved dependency graph, and built artifacts where relevant; focused automated tests with actual results; device or runtime observation for behavior static checks cannot prove; and only then PROJECT.md and supporting documents. Identify any review tooling, comments, tests, or code paths that incorrectly treat documentation as proof of runtime behavior.

Reviewer discipline for this question: AI reviewers can hallucinate, misread intent, or invent theoretical problems. Verify every claim against the actual current source, generated artifacts, resolved configuration, focused tests, and runtime evidence when applicable. It is completely acceptable to conclude that this area is correct and that no change is needed. Do not force a finding, manufacture a bug, or make speculative edits. Before changing anything, identify the exact file, symbol, call path, state transition, or artifact that proves the problem and explain the concrete failure scenario and impact. If a real issue is confirmed, make only the smallest safe correction and add or adjust focused regression coverage when it provides real value. Preserve existing behavior, StartEx safety boundaries, unrelated working-tree changes, and intentional limitations. Keep the code clean, direct, and as simple as possible. Avoid unnecessary abstractions, wrappers, helper layers, dependencies, broad refactors, or defensive complexity. Less is better.

## Q002. Verify that the whole checked-in project is included in the review boundary

Map every production, test, build, configuration, workflow, script, resource, schema, vendored binary, and generated-contract area that can affect StartEx. Check for overlooked source roots, variant-specific files, XML resources, native libraries, CI files, scanner suppressions, or external-script assumptions. Confirm that the review does not focus only on app/src/main/java while missing release, security, persistence, or operational behavior.

## Q003. Distinguish production-reachable behavior from scaffolds, tests, and dead code

Trace actual production composition and call sites before classifying a capability as active. In particular, separate Demo UI, Paper monitoring, real manual SOL transfer, disabled SPL transfer, hard-locked automated Live trading, and future Live safety scaffolds. Check whether any code review comment, test, naming choice, dependency injection path, reflection path, or UI callback could blur those boundaries.

## Q004. Validate every reported defect with an executable failure path

Inspect whether each candidate finding can be tied to a realistic entry point, reachable control flow, concrete input or state, and observable incorrect result. Reject findings that depend on impossible states, unreachable test-only scaffolds, undocumented assumptions, or generic best-practice claims with no StartEx impact. For concurrency or lifecycle issues, require a plausible event ordering rather than speculation.

## Q007. Avoid accidental mainnet side effects during review

Audit all suggested verification steps, tests, debug actions, and manual checks to ensure they cannot sign or broadcast automated trades or incidental real manual SOL transfers. Confirm that funded wallets, live provider credentials, or mainnet write endpoints are not required for ordinary review. ## Q009. Treat known limits as boundaries, not automatic defects

Review each known limitation and hotspot in PROJECT.md only in the context of actual correctness or safety. Confirm that the review does not automatically implement Live trading, SPL transfers, metadata services, user-editable retention, notification toggles, certificate pinning, boot restart, or other intentionally absent features. # 2. Product boundaries, operating modes, and side-effect separation

## Q011. Prove that Demo mode is fully local and side-effect free

Trace every Demo entry point, state producer, screen callback, ViewModel method, provider adapter, service intent, wallet operation, export action, and persistence observer. Verify that enabling or using Demo cannot create or unlock a real wallet, restore provider keys, start monitoring, call network providers, write real trading history, sign transactions, submit transfers, export real data, or leak stale real state into the synthetic UI.
